- Root `CLAUDE.md`, "Architecture": `plugin/detect/` (the detection statistics, the routes and runbooks, the dismissal store, the (f) CLI), `plugin/evals/` (the eval runner and the incident case skeleton), `plugin/scan/` (the weekly security review and the scanners) — three lines to add; and `template/`: `lessons/`, the detect, runbook, scan and evals workflows. **Done 2026-09-25**; the three `plugin/` lines (24–26) lack the backticks of their neighbours — cosmetic, the owner's choice.
- Still optional from session 4: `gate.max_panel_calls: 4` in the root `sdlc.yaml`; the template's withdrawal note in place of its `# lite:` comment; the sample's `CLAUDE.md` Layout line for `mean`.

## Fresh-context review (Opus, between the two commits of the PR)
The review read the whole diff against the handoff, the decisions and the operating model, and reported sixteen findings. Every one was fixed in the second commit, with a test where the review said none existed; the two it judged low and left as is are recorded in the known gaps:
1. **The scheduled run never ran** (critical): the workflow passes `--force-tier ""` on a schedule, which the parser refused. Fixed (`""` = force nothing) and tested; the template test that had locked the run line in now also asserts the empty value.
2. **The model could change a route's authorization, and deploy secrets sat in its environment** (high): the dispatcher ran inside the model's session and read the checkout's `bands.yaml`. Fixed: in CI the dispatch, gate (f) and the PR are a step of their own after the model's (`detect/cli.py finish`; the runbook secrets live in that step's `env:` only; the model's session runs `/sdlc-maintain <id> --diagnosis-only`), and the dispatcher judges by the default branch's `bands.yaml` and `sdlc.yaml` through `git show`, never the checkout's (choice 80). Gate (f)'s `route` check reads the merge base's `bands.yaml` when the branch changed it.
3. **A rehearsal could trigger a real rollback** (high): `force_tier: 3` plus decision 26. Fixed: a forced finding never pre-approves a runbook (`routes.apply_forced`; choice 81).
4. **Merging a parked incident PR started nothing** (high): the design guard refused a change at `f` with a park. Fixed: the merge lifts the park (`run_phase.guard`) and `read_status(on_default_branch=True)` reads a merged `f` as gate (a) passed (`merged_at_gate_a`).
5. **The built-in runbooks failed on a declared production** (medium-high): the hook log the production gate appends to made the tree "dirty". Fixed: files under `changes/` never block a runbook.
6. **A partial day was judged** (medium): the 05:41 UTC run compared today's first hours with full days. Fixed: only complete UTC days are judged (choice 82).
7. **The same finding came back** (medium): a merged incident whose head GitHub deleted, and a closed one whose dismissal PR was not merged yet. Fixed: `open_incidents` also reads the default branch's incident folders, and a pending `sdlc/<id>/dismiss` branch already suppresses the signature (choice 83).
8. **The digest missed the runbook and dismissal PRs** (medium): they carry `incident` and no gate label. Fixed: a "Phase (f): runbook and dismissal PRs" section; the dismissal PR is labelled `incident`.
9. **Two counters were zero by construction** (medium): abandoned and dismissed never reach the default branch. Fixed: the rows the default branch cannot tell were dropped and the section says so.
10. **This repository's copy of the eval workflow was always red** (medium): no `evals/check.py` here. Fixed: the copy removed; `framework-evals.yml` is this repository's; the template test says so.
11. **The Go and the route check were too trusting** (medium-low). Fixed: `go` runs only a route whose record says `go-requested`; a `ran` record on a `go` route needs `authorized_by`, a shipped runbook's needs its PR or a reason.
12. **A stray hook log was committed** (low): a test run before the test fixture existed left `changes/.hook-log.jsonl`. Fixed: removed, ignored here (`.gitignore`), the fixture `tests/conftest.py` keeps every test's log in its temp dir.
13. **Eval workspaces carried the framework checkout** (low). Fixed: `framework/` ignored.
14. **Case 0003 tested the behaviour, not the hook** (low). Fixed by saying so in the case: the credential must not land whichever half refuses.
15. **`dismiss` on a detached HEAD or a second close** (low). Fixed: the restore point is the commit when detached; a pending `origin/sdlc/<id>/dismiss` is continued.
16. **Docs** (low): NOTES §13c said `upload-artifact` had no v5 without a source (rewritten as an unverified choice to check on the next bump); OPERATING_MODEL §4.1 lacked `Bash(gh run list *)` (added). The review confirmed the statistics against a hand-computed series, the decisions in the routing code, the token rule in every workflow, the credentials only on the model steps, and that a maintain run cannot commit source and pass.

A red commit slipped out after the review: the second commit (e7fd9dd) was pushed on the tail of the test log instead of the exit code — the mistake the handoff names — while its run had one failure (`test_security_baseline_check_finds_secrets_and_dangerous_calls`: a comment line of the new detect workflow, "holds no deploy secret: a project", matched the secrets hook's credential-assignment pattern on the initialised fixture), and its message claims 1103 passed. The third commit rewords the comment, and its checks below are from a run whose exit code was read.

## Left (no build stage remains)
- The live checks 16–23 and the owed 8; the deploy/status/rollback tools when an adapter names a real deployment (choice 35); the `Diff.files` conversion; the per-decision panel cost; an owner `accept` verb for a review finding (a new decision, if wanted); the release-page backfill for v0.2.15–v0.2.17 (optional); a runbook for a Node flaky test when a Node project needs one; step 33 (decision 17) and step 40 (optional). The session-6 `HANDOFF.md` was written from this list: the live checks are its work.

# Session 4 record (B4: release gate, deploy adapters, decisions 21–26; steps 29, 31, 32, 16a) — kept as written at the end of session 4 (B4: release gate, deploy adapters, then decisions 21–26; steps 29, 31, 32, 16a)

## Summary
- Session 4 closed on 2026-09-24 with PRs H (docs: the branch migration), I (release pages + the cross-phase ledger, 0.2.18) and J (docs: live check 15) merged; `main` at 0.2.18, tagged and released by the workflow, the root and the sample pins applied. 725 tests. Live-proven on the sample repository: the deferred-review design (10), the overturn (11), the repeated reset by its stamp and the default-branch pin (13), the first phase (c) under deferred review (14) and the first unattended (c) → (d) → (e) chain (15). `HANDOFF.md` is the session-5 brief (B5); the session-4 brief is `docs/handoffs/session-4-B4.md`.
- PR A of session 4 is built and green on the owner's Windows PC: `python tasks.py check` passes with 657 tests in 17.5 minutes (ruff zero warnings, 112 files formatted) and `claude plugin validate .` is clean; it is one draft PR on `claude/b4-release-gate` against `main`. The plugin is version **0.2.12: the owner tags the merge commit `v0.2.12` and bumps this repository's root `sdlc.yaml` pin** (a guardrail file) — this repository's own design, build and release workflows now call `run_phase.py --find-change` and `release/cli.py`, which no earlier tag has, so the merge of PR A itself fires one design job that ends red at 0.2.11 (expected, harmless) and every later merge is green once the tag and the pin exist.
- Gate (e) is finished (step 32; decision 13): the owner's merge counts as gate (e) passed without any write to `main` (`state/status.py read_status(..., on_default_branch=True)` derives it; `state/cli.py show|list` and the release workflow read it that way — the sample repository's change 0001 reads as passed from `main` with no edit); `/sdlc-deploy` prepares the release artifact per the adapter before the review pass and writes `evidence/release-notes.md` (`plugin/release/notes.py`, deterministic) after the gate; `template/.github/workflows/sdlc-release.yml` runs `sdlc.yaml: deploy.command` on the owner's merge of the build PR (and, with `deploy.production: true`, only once the release approval exists — it listens to `closed` and `labeled` because the label may come after the merge); installed by `/sdlc-init` and mirrored into this repository's `.github/workflows/`.
- The production gate exists (step 29): `plugin/hooks/production_gate.py` (PreToolUse on `Bash|PowerShell`) blocks the adapter's guarded commands, `sdlc.yaml: deploy.guarded_commands` and the article's `deploy` + `production` trigger until the approval exists — the label `sdlc:release-approved` applied by a person on GitHub, read from the PR's timeline and never from an environment variable (`plugin/release/approval.py`, shared with the release workflow; a signed tag was rejected because a session with `git` can sign one itself); the hook also blocks any command that names the label, so a run cannot apply it to itself; allow or block, never ask; fail closed; exit 2 with the reason and the route; inert when `deploy.production` is false; every decision one timestamped JSON line in `changes/<id>-<slug>/evidence/hook-log.jsonl` (`_common.log_decision`, `SDLC_HOOK_LOG` overrides). Deploy/status/rollback tools are not built (step 31): the interface is recorded in OPERATING_MODEL §9 and built when a project's adapter names a real deployment; DORA measures come from the CI system and the deployment tooling (p.41).
- The live-check gaps of session 3 are closed in the plumbing: the merge-triggered design and build workflows find the change from the merged PR's files (`run_phase.py --find-change`, a 20-second first step; a web-session intent PR with a `claude/…` head now starts the design run), `commit-phase` stages and commits only the files that changed under its paths, `actions/checkout` and `actions/setup-node` are on v5 everywhere; and three carried gaps: `_start_point` uses `sdlc/<id>/b` only in the Lite profile, `guardrails` and `risk_list` judge the committed diff like the other checks (the gate matches OPERATING_MODEL §3), and the adversarial reviewer lost `Bash` — the run writes `evidence/diff-<phase>.patch` and passes HEAD's sha (a scoped `Bash(git *)` is not a documented sub-agent tool value, NOTES §11c).
- PR B (plugin **0.2.13**; the owner tags its merge commit `v0.2.13` and bumps the root `sdlc.yaml` pin) builds decisions 22, 24, 25 and 23. The owner's "Request changes" review starts the fix round by itself (`template/.github/workflows/sdlc-fix.yml` on `pull_request_review` `submitted` with `changes_requested`, a person as reviewer → `run_phase.py --phase fix` on the PR's own head → `/sdlc-fix <id>`, which now covers gate (a): it corrects `intent.md` on the unmerged intent PR, the one exception to "nothing in a run edits intent.md"). The owner's un-park verbs are labels (`sdlc:accept-risk`, `sdlc:reset-iterations`, `sdlc:unlock-tests`): they fire the same workflow, the run performs them first (`plugin/state/unpark.py`), records the label's actor in `status.yaml` (`risk_accepted_by`, `iterations_reset_by`, `tests_unlocked_by`), removes the label and re-runs the phase; the gate's `owner_actions` check honours a recorded person and rejects a `[bot]`. A PR closed without a merge abandons its change (`sdlc-abandon.yml` → `state/cli.py abandon --all-branches`: `phase: abandoned` on every branch of the change, branches kept, every guard skips it). `/sdlc-init` refuses an `origin` on a host other than github.com with the reason and lets a missing or local-path remote through with a `hosting` note; its upgrade path reports an installed workflow that differs from the template as `outdated`. Written into OPERATING_MODEL §1, 4, 4.1, 4.2, 5, 6, 8, 10. The first "Request changes" round, the first label and the first close are the owner's live checks (below). This PR was rebuilt in a cloud session after the owner's PC died mid-session on 2026-09-24 with the first build uncommitted (nothing of it had reached GitHub). PR C (plugin **0.2.14**; the owner tags its merge commit `v0.2.14` and bumps the pins) builds decision 21 and removes the Lite profile. `sdlc.yaml: review: parked | deferred` (per change `status.yaml: review_override`, off by default): under `deferred`, before each gate the run puts the judgment items of the fixed list to a review panel (`plugin/panel/`: `items` lists what the gate would park on — an open flagged concern, a concern naming contradicting policies, the reviewer's `escalate`, an Important finding the run cannot fix, the verifier's mismatch — and what stays a park; `prompt` prints the reviewer, devil's advocate and conciliator briefs for three fresh contexts, the advocate on `sdlc.yaml: panel_advocate_model`; `record` counts one iteration, writes the ledger `evidence/decisions-<phase>.json` + `.md` and closes a concern in `spec.md` as `decided (by panel): …`; `overturn` marks a line the owner's review comment reversed). The gate reads the ledger: an `escalate` decided `continue` and a finding decided `settled` pass, the new `panel` check refuses a ledger line without a decision or rationale, a panel closing with no ledger line, and any never-to-panel item; `pr/description.py` opens the PR with "Decisions taken for you (N)". Lite is gone from `conventions.PROFILES` (a file that still says it reads as Standard), `run_phase.py` (`NEXT_WORKFLOW["b"]`, `_start_point`), the commands, `template/sdlc.yaml` and the tests. Gaps 6(b) (the two `update` lines in the cloud setup script, NOTES §3a) and 6(f) (a finding about `CLAUDE.md` is proposed in the PR body the first time; the undeterminable finding goes to the panel) are closed. Everything after that is B5 (the list at the end of this section).
- PR D (plugin **0.2.15**; the owner asked for the tags to be created by the session from now on — the session cannot push a tag (NOTES §3b), so PR E adds `sdlc-tag.yml`, which tags `v<version>` on every merge to `main` that bumps `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, or on a `workflow_dispatch`; v0.2.15 is the first it creates, by a dispatch after PR E merges, since `main` already carries 0.2.15 untagged) is what the first live deferred run taught (sample change 0002, design job #14, 2026-09-24, green, $5.47, 22 min; the panel closed both concerns, the PR opened with "Decisions taken for you (2)"): **a panel call is not a fix iteration** — `status.yaml: panel_calls` against `gate.max_panel_calls` (default 4), the `limits` check parks past it, `sdlc:reset-iterations` resets both counts, the PR footer says `panel calls: N` (decision 21 amended, the owner's choice "separate counter"); **`record` commits the decision itself** (`design(<id>): panel decision <n>`; `--no-commit` for a caller that commits), so the verdict the reviewer writes afterwards is for a HEAD that carries it — the live run committed each verdict beside content the reviewer had not seen, three times, and needed four commits to land one; **the conciliator's decision is cleaned** (`ledger.clean_decision`: a copied `decided (by panel):` prefix and a restated item after an em dash are dropped; the brief no longer shows the closing format) — the live run's spec read `decided (by panel): decided (by panel): …`; **closings carry the ledger line** (`decided (by panel #n): …`) and the gate's `panel` check matches on `n`, so a tidied wording no longer loses the match (the live run reverted its own tidy for that); the adversarial reviewer is told the panel's files are never in the diff it judges (it escalated on "the panel did not run" against a pre-panel diff, prompted by an intent that described the panel's artifacts as an outcome — intents describe the product, not the process).
- PR F (plugin **0.2.16**; tagged by `sdlc-tag.yml` on merge) is what the first overturn (live check 11, sample PR #13, 2026-09-24) taught. The overturn itself works end to end: the owner's "Request changes" fired the fix job in seconds, the first round parked honestly on the 0.2.14-era count (`iterations: 2` + 1 > cap 2, $0.49, nothing changed), `sdlc:reset-iterations` was performed by the label round (`iterations_reset_by: luissiviero`, committed by the automation identity), the second round rewrote the concern as `decided (owner, overturning the panel …): Collection[float]`, updated `plan.md`, marked ledger line 2 `overturned`, and the PR opened with "Decisions taken for you (1) — 1 overturned by your review comments". It then parked on an `escalate` the adversarial reviewer raised against the *previous* round's `claude-fix.json` ("proof mismatch": the transcript said nothing was applied, the diff applied everything). Three fixes: **every run keeps its own transcript** (`run_phase.run_record_paths`: `claude-<phase>.json`, then `claude-<phase>-2.json`, …), **the reviewer is told the runner's records are never evidence about the diff** (`evidence/claude-*`, `run-*.json` are stored after a run ends), and **`/sdlc-fix` gets the deferred-review panel step** (its `escalate` should have gone to the panel) plus a cap park that goes through the gate, so the PR gets `sdlc:needs-human` and "What I need from you" (the first round's `state park` left `sdlc:b-ready` and no block).
- PR G (plugin **0.2.17**) is what live check 12 taught, and it is the finding of the day: **a phase branch ran the framework it was created under.** Every workflow read the pin from the PR head's `sdlc.yaml` (`sdlc_pin.py` on the checkout), so `sdlc/0002/b`, created under 0.2.14, ran 0.2.14 through all the 0.2.15 and 0.2.16 "live checks" on it — the counters, the `#n` closings and the per-run transcripts never reached that branch. Now every workflow passes `--ref origin/<default branch>` and the script reads the default branch's copy (fetched at depth 1 when the checkout lacks it): the pin is the project's, like the configuration the gate already reads from the base. The second finding: a second `sdlc:reset-iterations` by the same person parked on `owner_actions` (the actor field did not change in the commit that lowered the count); `iterations_reset_at` is recorded with each reset and the check reads the stamp as the record of the act. The sample repository needs the new script and workflows (an `outdated` report from `/sdlc-init`; the session pushes them).

- PR I (plugin **0.2.18**): `sdlc-tag.yml` also creates the GitHub Release page for the tag it makes (`sdlc_tag.py create_release`: generated notes, once, never fatal; output `release=<url>|exists|skipped|failed:<status>`), because the Releases page showed v0.2.14 as "latest" while v0.2.15–v0.2.17 were tags only (the owner's question of 2026-09-24). v0.2.15–v0.2.17 stay tag-only. Live check 14 in passing: the build job of change 0002 (phase (c) under deferred review, on 0.2.17): the implementation, the plan reconciled with the formatter's import choice, the verifier's report, one panel call (the reviewer's `escalate` — it wanted phase (d)'s `test.log`/`build.log`/`lint.log` at (c) — decided `continue`, committed by `record` as `build(0002): panel decision 1`, `panel_calls: 1`), verdict `continue`, `claude-c.json`; then gate (c) parked on the `panel` check: "spec.md closes a concern by the panel with no ledger line" — the concern was closed at (b) and its line is in `decisions-b.json`, which the check read only for the phase's own ledger. Fixed here (choice 65): the check reads the ledgers of every phase so far for the closings in `spec.md`. The build PR waits at `sdlc:needs-human` until the 0.2.18 pin reaches the sample and a fix round re-runs the gate.
    Live check 15 (2026-09-24, 0.2.18, the first unattended chain): a `workflow_dispatch` of `sdlc-fix.yml` (`change_id: 0002`, `head_ref: sdlc/0002/c`; no label, so the counts stayed) re-ran gate (c) → `continue` (`panel: 1 panel decision(s) recorded`, the (b) closing found in `decisions-b.json`) → the test job dispatched itself → phase (d) in five minutes (`test.log`, `build.log`, `lint.log`, verifier, no panel item, gate (d) `continue`) → the deploy job dispatched itself → phase (e): the review pass raised the "CLAUDE.md outdated" finding (second occurrence, the proposed line in `evidence/claude-md-proposals.md`), the run committed `changes/.review-seen.json` itself (the `clean_tree` park item cleared), the panel settled the finding (`review(0002): panel decision 1`, `panel_calls: 2`), and gate (e) ended `wait` / `sdlc:e-ready` with the release note, `findings: 1 Important finding(s) settled by the review panel, none open`. The owner merged the build PR (#17) at 18:49, before the deploy job finished (19:03): the merge is gate (e) (decision 13), so `main` carries the change at phase (e) with everything through the review pass, and the release workflow ran green; the (e) panel decision, `gate-e.json` and the release note landed on the merged branch only. Nothing on `main` is wrong; a run's late commits after a merge are simply not in it — the PR body says `sdlc:e-ready` is the moment to merge.

## Decisions: none reopened, two interpretations recorded
Decision 13 is executed (merge = gate (e); the release label only on a declared production), decision 19 (the adapters) drives `/sdlc-deploy` step 0a and `deploy.command`, decision 7 and OPERATING_MODEL §8 (no third-party runtime dependency) are why step 31 is documented and not built, decision 11 (owner-only state) is why a `[bot]` actor never approves *[session 13: the citation is decision 5, the automation identity; decision 11 is park-never-page — corrected in the contract and the code, this record kept as written]*. Two readings the handoff makes of the decisions' text, built as the handoff says and recorded here for the owner to overrule if wanted: (i) decision 23 says `/sdlc-init` "refuses a project whose `origin` is not GitHub"; PR B refuses a non-GitHub host and lets a missing `origin` or a local-path remote through with "hosting: not GitHub (...): the CI plumbing will not run" (the fixture tests push to a local bare remote); (ii) decision 22 says the fix trigger finds the change "as HANDOFF item 4(a) settles"; PR B reads the change from the PR's own files, because an unmerged intent PR has no `status.yaml` on the default branch (4(a)'s lookup, built in PR A, is for merges). A third, from decision 24's "read by the next run": (iii) the un-park labels do not wait for whichever run comes next — each label fires `sdlc-fix.yml`, because a reset or an acceptance is only useful when the phase re-runs, and the fix round is the run that re-runs a phase on the owner's input; a label the owner applies with no review comment is a fix round whose only change request is the park it lifts.

## Choices made where the pack left it open (continuing session 3's numbering)
31. **What counts as release approval** (29.1): the label `sdlc:release-approved` on the build PR applied by a person — the actor of the last `labeled` event, read through the PR's timeline, never from an environment variable; a label applied by the automation identity or any `<app>[bot]` account never counts, and a removed label no longer counts. The handoff and build guide step 29 also offered "a signed tag on the merge commit"; the fresh-context review showed it is not owner-only (a session allowed `git *` can create a key or set the gpg config and `git tag -s` HEAD, and on the owner's PC the signing key is already configured), so the tag route was removed before the commit: the label is the one form, and the hook blocks any command that names the label (`gh pr edit --add-label`, the REST endpoint, a script) — decision 11, a run cannot approve itself *[session 13: decision 5]*. The hook only allows or blocks: the run is unattended, so "ask" would hang it; the block text carries the route.
32. **Both `closed` and `labeled` fire the release workflow** (32.3): the owner may apply the release label after merging, when the `closed` event has fired and found no approval (a green `waiting` job); the `labeled` event, filtered to `sdlc:release-approved`, re-runs the check. Whether GitHub fires `labeled` on a merged PR is not stated in the docs (NOTES §11a); the design is correct either way and a `workflow_dispatch(pr_number)` is the fallback.
33. **Release preparation in two halves** (32.1): the adapter's artifact (version bump, regenerated report, schedule entry, promotion record, manifest) is committed *before* the review pass, so the review and the gate judge HEAD with it — an artifact outside the change folder is listed in `plan.md` in the same commit or `commit-phase` refuses it; the release notes are evidence and are written *after* the gate into the evidence commit, because they quote the final findings tally and a second review pass per deploy run would double the (e) cost.
34. **A find step, not a head rule** (4(a)): every merged PR to the default branch starts the design and build workflows; the first step (`run_phase.py --find-change`, before any install, about 20 s) reads the PR's files and ends the job green when they touch no `changes/<id>-<slug>/` folder. A head that names another phase (`sdlc/<id>/b` merging into the design workflow) keeps its skip and is never looked up by its files, or the merge of a design PR would re-run the design.
35. **Deploy/status/rollback tools: not built now** (31.1). The plugin ships no runtime dependency, an MCP server would be one, and no project has a deploy target (this repository and the sample repository both say `deploy.action: none`). The interface is in OPERATING_MODEL §9 (three tools, every call through the production-gate hook, `rollback` pre-approved only under decision 26); `sdlc-release.yml` running `deploy.command` is the one deploy route until then.
36. **`deploy.command` runs without a shell**: one command, or steps joined by `&&`, globs expanded against the project root; `|`, `;`, `||` and redirections are refused as a configuration error (exit 2); a failing command is a red job (exit 1: infrastructure, not a park); the timeout is `gate.command_timeout`. GitHub environment protection stays the owner's: the template carries a commented `# environment: production` line.
37. **The adversarial reviewer without a shell** (6(e)): the sub-agents reference documents exact tool names and MCP patterns for `tools`, not permission-rule specifiers, so `Bash(git *)` cannot be given (NOTES §11c). The calling run writes `evidence/diff-<phase>.patch` (`--output=<file>`, one command, no redirection) and passes HEAD's sha; a brief without them makes the reviewer escalate; its "Proof" angle compares the committed evidence logs and `verifier.md` with the tests in the diff instead of running the toolchain (the verifier already did, p.27).
38. **The hook's time budget**: a PreToolUse command hook that reaches its timeout does not block the tool call (NOTES §8), so the production gate has a 60-second `timeout` and bounds each GitHub request and git call to 10 seconds with a 45-second deadline for the whole check; a timeout of its own is a block ("cannot verify the approval in time").
39. **The hook log lives with the change**: the default `changes/<id>-<slug>/evidence/hook-log.jsonl` when the branch names a change, because the gate's `clean_tree` parks on an untracked file outside the change folder and the evidence commit carries it; `changes/.hook-log.jsonl` is the fallback off a change branch; `SDLC_HOOK_LOG` overrides. Step 41's "kept with the change" is met; its "CI artifact" wording is B5's to reconcile when 41.1 extends the log to the other hooks.
40. **The release runs for the build PR only**: `release/cli.py` requires the merged PR's head to be `sdlc/<id>/c` (the branch the framework itself creates); a later owner PR that touches `changes/<id>-<slug>/` of a finished change (a `status.yaml` fix, say) is a skip, so a release never runs twice. Found by the fresh-context review.
41. **Gate (e) reads as passed on a declared production too**: the merge is the phase gate in every profile (decision 13: "merge is the whole gate"); the release approval is a release-level act the release workflow enforces, so `read_status(..., on_default_branch=True, production=True)` keeps `passed` and its reason names the second act. `status.yaml` does not track the release.
42. **The four-hour test run was environmental**: the first full run over PR A took 3 h 56 min for 621 tests against 13 min for 506 the same morning; measured afterwards, the new and changed test files run at about 1.3 s per test (the slowest, a `commit-phase` round trip, 9.5 s) and the final run alone on the machine took 17.5 min for 657 tests. During that run four build agents and the reviewer were running their own `pytest` suites on the same PC, each spawning git and Python subprocesses; the rule for the rest of the session is one full suite at a time.
43. **`project_dir` keeps the path's case**: the hooks' shared helper returned the lower-cased comparison form, so on Linux a project whose path has capitals would have lost `sdlc.yaml` (the test-file lock silently off, the protected list at its defaults); it now returns a filesystem-usable path and `norm()` stays for comparisons. Found while building the production gate; no live run had hit it (both repositories have lower-case paths).
44. **The fix round runs on the PR's own head, whatever it is called** (5(a)): `run_phase.py --phase fix` takes the head from the event (`--head-ref`) and switches to it without creating anything; `commit-phase --branch <head>` and `upsert --head <head>` follow it, so an intent PR a web session pushed from `claude/…` is corrected on that branch and refreshed as the same PR. The gate file, the spend record, the run record and the hand-over use the change's own phase (`gate_phase`), never "fix": a fix round is the phase re-run, and a `continue` at (c) or (d) dispatches the next phase as the phase run would. A review by the workflow token or any `[bot]` starts nothing (the job's `if`), so the round cannot loop on itself.
45. **A label is one act**: the run performs the label, records the actor, and removes the label from the PR; the `labeled` event stays in the timeline as the audit line, `status.yaml` carries the actor. Without the removal every later run would reset the count again and the iteration cap would mean nothing. A label the automation identity or a `[bot]` applied is rejected and left where it is, so the owner sees it. `sdlc:accept-risk` accepts the hits the last gate of the change's phase reported (`evidence/gate-<phase>.json`, check `risk_list`, `details.hits`): the label carries no item name, and that file is what the owner read in "What I need from you"; with no recorded hit there is nothing to accept and the label stays. The PR upsert never removes the owner's labels (the release approval and the three verbs) when it sets the gate label — until 0.2.13 it swept every `sdlc:` label but the gate's, which would have removed a release approval applied before a fix round.
46. **`risk_accepted_by` is a list of `{item, actor}`**, not a mapping: a risk item may carry a space (`data migrations`) and `state/yamlish.py` supports no quoted keys.
47. **Abandon marks every branch of the change** (5(c)): the closed PR's head first, then every other `sdlc/<id>/*` branch the checkout knows from its remote-tracking refs or `ls-remote`, each with its own commit under the automation identity; a later `workflow_dispatch` of any phase switches to one of those branches before its guard reads `status.yaml`, so it skips. The branches stay: nothing is deleted, the owner may reopen by hand (a new intent is the documented route). Dismissing an incident finding on the close of an incident intent PR waits for step 39 (B5): nothing detects yet.
48. **The hosting rule reads the URL's host only**: `https://`, `ssh://` and scp-style `git@host:path` remotes name a host; a local path, a Windows drive path or a `file://` URL name none and pass with the note (the fixture tests, an owner's local mirror). `github.com` and `*.github.com` are GitHub. `--detect-only` is refused the same way: a detection report for a project the framework will not install on would mislead.
49. **`outdated`, not `kept`**: the installer cannot tell an owner's edit from an older framework's copy, so an existing workflow that differs from this version's template is reported as `outdated: differs from what plugin <version> installs; replace it ... or keep your edits` and the command shows those first. Replacing is the owner's act (delete the file and re-run `/sdlc-init`), never the installer's: a workflow is project content.
50. **The abandon step's reason has no `#`**: a `#` after a space starts a YAML comment even inside a `run:` scalar; `sdlc-abandon.yml` first lost the tail of its run line to it, and `tests/test_templates.py` now parses every workflow and compares the run line.
51. **The panel runs after the verdict and before the gate, and the verdict is re-run when a decision changed a file** (16a): the items come from a dry-run gate (`panel/cli.py items`), so an `escalate` verdict is an item; a concern the panel closes changes `spec.md`, the run commits it and the verdict is taken again for the new HEAD (a verdict never outlives the diff it judged). An `escalate` is keyed by its reasons, not by the head: the same reasons on the new HEAD are the item already decided, new reasons are a new item (bounded by the iteration cap, one per panel call).
52. **The three members are sub-agents by hand and in CI alike**: the phase run's session delegates the reviewer and the conciliator to general-purpose sub-agents and the devil's advocate to `sdlc:adversarial-reviewer` with a per-invocation model (`sdlc.yaml: panel_advocate_model`, default `opus`; NOTES §11c: the per-invocation model wins), instead of three separate `claude -p` jobs: the CI job already is one `claude -p` run with `Agent` allowed, the cost lands in the same `total_cost_usd`, and the brief, not the transport, is what makes the members blind. The cost per decision is recorded when the run knows it (`record --cost-usd`), else `n/a`.
53. **A panel decision is a concern closed with `decided (by panel): <decision> — <concern>`**: the closing word first, so the existing `open_concerns` check and `/sdlc-fix`'s rule ("the closing word is the first word") hold unchanged; the original text stays on the line so the owner reads what was decided about what; the `panel` gate check ties every such item to a ledger line.
54. **The ledger is JSON with a rendered `.md`**: the gate, the PR summary and `/sdlc-fix` read `decisions-<phase>.json`; `decisions-<phase>.md` is the same ledger for a person (one line per decision, the two verdicts in one clause each, the decision, the rationale, the cost, "OVERTURNED" when the owner reversed it). Both are evidence, committed with the phase.
55. **`lite` in an old file reads as `standard`** (`conventions.LEGACY_PROFILES`): the sample repository and this repository never used Lite, but a `status.yaml` written before 0.2.14 may say it, and a validation error on read would have parked every reader.
56. **`review` is a top-level key, per project, with a per-change override**: the decision's wording; `/sdlc-init --review`, `new-change --review-override`. Upgrading a project adds the key through the installer's usual text-edit path (`missing_top`), default `parked`.
57. **Panel calls are their own count** (decision 21 amended in 0.2.15, the owner's choice): `status.yaml: panel_calls`, `sdlc.yaml: gate.max_panel_calls` (default 4, whatever the classification), `limits` parks past it with the two ways out (the label, or the key in a reviewed PR), `gate/cli.py set-iterations --panel-calls N` by hand, `record_owner_label(sdlc:reset-iterations)` zeroes both. The alternative, keeping one count and raising `max_iterations_non_routine`, would have made one budget cover two unrelated things.
58. **`record` commits, the verdict comes last**: `panel/cli.py record` commits the change folder on the current branch (`gitops.commit_paths`, the automation identity when the checkout has none) right after applying the decision; the runbooks re-run the verdict once after the last item and say that nothing but the gate evidence is committed after it. Prose alone did not hold: the live run followed "commit, then re-run the verdict" by committing the verdict file with the content it had not judged.
59. **The closing names its ledger line**: `decided (by panel #n): <decision> — <concern>`; `ledger.panel_closings` reads `n` (the 0.2.14 form without it is still read and matched on the decision text), `check_panel` refuses a number the ledger lacks. Matching on the decision's words was one tidy away from a park.
60. **One transcript per run** (`claude-<phase>.json`, `claude-<phase>-2.json`, …; `run_phase.run_record_paths`): the runner stores a transcript after the run ends, so during a fix round the only committed one is the previous round's; overwriting it would lose the record and keeping one name misled the reviewer. Numbered names keep every run and say which is which.
61. **The runner's records are not proof** (adversarial-reviewer.md): `evidence/claude-*` and `run-*.json` describe the run that produced them, never the diff a later reviewer judges; the proof the reviewer compares is the toolchain output and the artifacts. The alternative — teaching the runner to store the transcript before the verdict — is impossible: the transcript is the run.
62. **A fix round is a phase run** (`sdlc-fix.md` step 5): under deferred review its judgment items, the reviewer's `escalate` included, go to the panel before its gate, with the same block as the phase runbooks; and its iteration-cap park runs `gate/cli.py check` (the `limits` check writes the gate file, the label and the block) rather than `state/cli.py park`, because the PR's label and body come from the gate file.
63. **The pin is read from the default branch** (`sdlc_pin.py --ref origin/<default>`, every workflow): a phase branch's `sdlc.yaml` is a snapshot, and a pull request may outlive several releases; the run's configuration already comes from the base branch's copy, so the framework version does too. The alternative — merging the default branch into the PR branch before each run — would put a merge commit into every round and still not help a by-hand run.
64. **A reset is recorded by its stamp** (`status.yaml: iterations_reset_at`, set by `record_owner_label`): the `owner_actions` check reads the commit that lowered the count for a recorded act, and a second reset by the same person changes the stamp only. Reading the label event from GitHub instead would need a token in the gate, which by-hand runs do not have.
65. **A closing is matched against the ledgers of every phase so far** (`ledger.phases_up_to`, `load_ledgers`; `check_panel`): `spec.md`'s concerns are closed at (b) and stay closed, so a gate at (c), (d) or (e) finds the line in `decisions-b.json`. Validation, the never-to-panel rule and the mode rule still apply to the phase's own ledger; `escalate` and `finding` entries stay per phase.

Still owed from session 3 (unreported at the start of session 4; nothing in PR A depends on them): the sample repository's watch setting ("Participating and @mentions"); the test-file lock denial seen live on Windows (the hook's 103 stdin tests pass here; only Claude Code's own spawn of the hook is unproven); the agent-trigger check ("run the adversarial reviewer for phase c of change 0001" spawns `sdlc:adversarial-reviewer`); `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=opus` in the cloud environment; whether the sample repository's change-0000 PR #4 was merged. Then `python "<plugin>/plugin/state/cli.py" show --id 0001` on `main` should print `gate: e/passed (derived: merged on main)`.

Four defects, all fixed in PR D (choices 57–59 and the reviewer note): the two calls exhausted the non-routine cap, the verdict was committed stale three times, decision 2 carried a doubled `decided (by panel):` prefix, and a tidy of the closing lines broke the ledger match. The overturn half of this check waits for 0.2.15 (live check 11).
11.     Result (2026-09-24, 0.2.15): done by the session (the review, then the `sdlc:reset-iterations` label). Round 1 parked on the legacy count, the label round reset it, round 2 applied the overturn (spec, plan, ledger line 2 overturned, `iterations: 1`, "Decisions taken for you (1) — 1 overturned"), then parked on the reviewer's "proof mismatch" escalate against the previous round's transcript. The second round, on the merged head, ran 0.2.17: the label commit carries `panel_calls: 0` and `iterations_reset_at`, the round wrote `claude-fix-2.json` beside `claude-fix.json`, `owner_actions` credited the reset by its stamp, the reviewer said `continue`, gate (b) `wait`, PR #13 back at `sdlc:b-ready` with "Decisions taken for you (1) — 1 overturned". ## Known gaps (carried to PR B, PR C and B5)
- In CI the deploy workflow's review job runs before `/sdlc-deploy`; for a project whose adapter is not `none` the step-0a artifact commit makes that review's `head` stale and the run re-reviews once by delegation. No project has such an adapter yet; when the first one does, the workflow should prepare the artifact before its review job (a model-driven step, so it is a second `claude -p` job, not a deterministic pre-step).
- Closed in PR B: `/sdlc-init` now reports an installed workflow that differs from the template as `outdated` (choice 49); replacing it is still the owner's act.
- The un-park labels and the fix trigger need a route to GitHub in the run (`GITHUB_TOKEN` in CI, `gh` by hand): without one `apply_owner_labels` reads nothing and says so in the run's JSON (`owner_labels.reason`), and the round proceeds on the comments alone. A fix round in a Full-profile project reads no approval label: the approval gates the *next* phase, not the re-run of the current one.
- Dismissing the finding on the close of an incident intent PR (decision 25, second half) waits for step 39 (B5): `abandon` records the phase only; nothing detects yet.
- `gate/diff.Diff.files` (the working tree) is still read by `approved_artifact`, by `gate.py`'s check for an `sdlc.yaml` changed on the branch and by `policy.py`; the four diff-judging checks read HEAD. Convert them when one of those files is next touched.
- Monthly tuning of review findings (26.5): B5. Step 41's hook-log wording: reconciled by 41.1 in B5. Full-profile approval label without `gh` or a token degrades with a printed note: unchanged, no Full-profile project in sight.
- Closed in PR C: the "CLAUDE.md outdated" finding is proposed in the PR body the first time (`claude_md_first_time_lines`) and, under deferred review, the panel may settle it so gate (e) passes with the finding recorded; under `review: parked` it still parks, by design. An owner `accept` verb for findings (a label or a comment that waives one Important finding without the panel) would be a new decision: proposed here, not built.
- The panel's cost is recorded only when the run passes `--cost-usd` (a by-hand run has no figure per sub-agent); in CI the three members' spend is inside the job's `total_cost_usd`, which `record-spend` books to the phase. A per-decision cost needs the sub-agent's own cost, which `/tasks` shows but no CLI output carries: B5, with step 41's observability.
- The panel's kinds cover the fixed list; "a verifier disagreement" is read from `evidence/verifier.md`'s verdict line (`does not match the plan`) and "contradicting policies" from the concern's wording (`contradict`/`conflict` + `policy`/`skill`). Both are lexical; a spec that words them otherwise makes them plain concerns, which the panel takes anyway.
- Nothing builds a per-phase iteration reset (6(g): the counter is per change by contract) and "one shell command per Bash call" stays prose (6(h)).

- New in session 4's live checks: (i) a `labeled` event on a pull request ran the head's copies of the workflow, the pin script and the pin (NOTES §3c) — a phase branch created before 0.2.17 is migrated by one merge of the default branch; whether GitHub reads the workflow file from the head or from a stale merge ref could not be settled (the Actions logs host is outside the session's network policy); (ii) GitHub's review widget keeps "changes requested" after a fix round applied the comment — dismissing it is the owner's, since re-requesting a review is a notification (decision 20); (iii) a build PR merged before its deploy job ends leaves the (e) panel decision, `gate-e.json` and the release note on the merged branch only (decision 13 makes the merge the gate; `main` stays consistent); (iv) v0.2.15–v0.2.17 are tags without release pages (the release page came in 0.2.18; a backfill is one `workflow_dispatch` with a version argument, if wanted).

# Session 3 record (B3: first autonomous phases, gates, merge-triggered plumbing; steps 22–30) — kept as written at the end of session 3

## Session 3 summary
- B3 is built and green: `python tasks.py check` passes with 432 tests (ruff zero warnings) on Linux and `claude plugin validate .` is clean; the work is one draft PR (#10) on `main`. The plugin is now version 0.2.0: **the owner tags the merge commit `v0.2.0`**, because the phase workflows check the framework out at `v<pin>`.
- The phase commands exist: `/sdlc-design` (the article's p.14 prompt, revised from three by-hand runs, with the five policy skills loaded, a `spec-template` skill, the read-only planning run, the adversarial verdict and gate (b)), the runbooks `/sdlc-build`, `/sdlc-test`, `/sdlc-deploy` composed in OPERATING_MODEL §4.1 over deterministic Python (`evidence/collect.py`, `state/cli.py commit-phase` on the shared build branch, `gate/cli.py bump-iteration`), and `/sdlc-fix` as the single change-request handler. Every run stops at a human gate with the ready label and parks on any gate failure; nothing notifies.
- The guardrails and reviews grew: the test-file lock hook for fix-type changes (`status.yaml: tests_locked`, `sdlc.yaml: test_paths`), the gate's `design_scope` and `owner_actions` checks (a run cannot accept its own risk hit or reset its own iteration count), the fresh-context review pass (`review/cli.py prompt | validate`, findings with signatures, the second-occurrence rule producing proposed `CLAUDE.md` lines), and the PR summary builder (`pr/cli.py upsert`, ≤5 bullets from the gate file) with a daily digest into a pinned issue (decision 20: nothing pushes).
- The merge-triggered plumbing is in the template: five workflows (`sdlc-design`, `sdlc-build`, `sdlc-test`, `sdlc-deploy`, `sdlc-digest`) installed by `/sdlc-init`, each running `ci/run_phase.py` — guard on `status.yaml`, pinned framework via `--plugin-dir`, `claude -p` with the run limits, `--permission-prompts none`, the CI sandbox settings, either credential (key preferred), spend recorded, the next phase dispatched explicitly (workflow-token events never start runs). The minimum Claude Code version is 2.1.278.
- Two fresh-context Opus reviews (code, compliance vs the brief and the decisions) ran before the final commit: 6 Important code findings and 5 blocking compliance findings were fixed (choices 26–30; the `/sdlc-init` upgrade path, the durable CI park, the workflow injection surface, the fail-open approval check, the concurrency key, the wall clock in `/sdlc-fix`).
- Left for B4: the production-gate hook and what counts as release approval (step 29), the deployment-tooling decision (31), release notes and the release workflow on the owner's merge (32); plus the owner's live checks below, above all the smoke run and the first end-to-end design run on the sample repository.

## Session 3 · Decisions: none reopened, none impossible
Decision 1 (CI with either credential, key preferred) and decision 12 (review pass as a CI job) are executed by `ci/run_phase.py` and `sdlc-deploy.yml` (review job, then the phase run). Decision 20's "nothing pushes" is honoured by the digest medium (below).

## Session 3 · Choices made where the pack left it open
16. **Digest medium** (27a.0): one pinned issue titled "SDLC review queue" whose body the daily job rewrites; the owner sets the repository watch to "Participating and @mentions". The GitHub docs do not state whether body edits notify, so the design does not rely on it: the issue is opened by the workflow token and the owner never comments on it (NOTES §11a).
17. **Owner-only state** (24.5): `accept-risk` and `set-iterations` stay CLI commands; the gate's `owner_actions` check honours a risk acceptance or an iteration reset only when the commit that introduced it is not authored by the automation identity (`sdlc.yaml: automation_identity`, default `github-actions[bot]`), and parks on an uncommitted one. By-hand runs are the owner's own sessions, so their commits count as the owner's act.
18. **Sandbox for phase jobs** (30.7): the GitHub-hosted runner (ephemeral VM, only the CI credential) plus Claude Code's sandbox with `failIfUnavailable: true` and an allowed-domain list (`plugin/ci/settings.ci.json`, passed with `--settings` because bare mode reads no settings); `ci/runner_setup.py` installs bubblewrap and socat and the AppArmor profile when needed. `permissions:` per job: contents, pull-requests, issues, checks, actions — which endpoint needs which could not be verified from the reachable docs (NOTES §11a); the first live run verifies.
19. **Test-file lock semantics** (25.1): as the handoff proposed — `status.yaml: tests_locked` set by the build run after the reproducing test is committed; `sdlc.yaml: test_paths` with defaults per detected language; the lock holds on `sdlc/<id>/c` through (d) and (e); only the owner unlocks.
20. **Lite profile at (b)**: spec+plan are committed on `sdlc/<id>/b` (no PR) and `/sdlc-build` starts `sdlc/<id>/c` from that branch, so branch names keep one meaning.
21. **A park at (b) still opens the PR** with `sdlc:needs-human` and the "What I need from you" block: the queue is the PR list (OPERATING_MODEL §7), and the by-hand runs showed 6–9 open concerns per first spec, so the first (b) PR is usually a parked one whose work is closing concerns (article p.14 step 4).
22. **Verdict and gate ordering** in every phase run: commit the artifacts → the adversarial reviewer judges HEAD → the gate records → the evidence is committed. A later re-check sees a stale verdict by design; each run produces its own.
23. **Minimum Claude Code version 2.1.278** (the pinned CLI, `sdlc.yaml: plugin.claude_code`), since `--permission-prompts none` needs 2.1.259.
24. **Review in CI is its own `claude -p` job** (`run_phase.py --phase review`: the prompt from `review/cli.py prompt`, Read/Grep/Glob/git and one Write), then the (e) phase run finds the findings file for HEAD and validates it instead of delegating (decision 12's "writer ≠ judge").
25. **A park is a green job**: `run_phase.py` exits 0 on `park` (it is a queue item, not a CI failure) and 1 only on infrastructure failures (the CLI failing, no gate file after the run).
26. **`/sdlc-fix` never comments** (decision 20): a fix round records what it did per request in `evidence/fix-response.md`, committed with the evidence and visible in the PR diff; it posts no reply, review, assignment or @mention. Every round also calls `start-run` first, so the wall clock of the previous run does not park the round.
27. **CI refuses a phase on a branch that changed a guardrail file** (`run_phase.py` guard; decision 6 layer iii as prevention): `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `REVIEW.md`, `sdlc.yaml` or a protected path differing from the base parks the change before `claude -p` starts, unless the merged intent says `Framework change: yes`. The residual exposure is documented in OPERATING_MODEL §4.2 and NOTES §11a: whether Claude Code honours a `hooks` or `env` block from an untrusted project checkout is not documented.
28. **A CI park is durable**: `run_phase.py` creates or switches to the phase's work branch before the guard, and a park commits `status.yaml` there, pushes and upserts the PR with `sdlc:needs-human`, so the queue item exists even on an ephemeral runner.
29. **Phase (b) in CI runs without Edit tools** (`--disallowedTools Edit,MultiEdit,NotebookEdit`); `Write` cannot be path-scoped (NOTES §6), so the `design_scope` gate check remains the net against a source file written in (b). The command's allow rule is `Write` (unscoped) for the same reason.
30. **A failed job is red, a park is green**: GitHub e-mails failed Actions runs by default; that is the one push left, and it signals an infrastructure failure, not a gate. The owner may turn it off in GitHub's notification settings. The digest's first run opens the pinned issue and may notify once.

## Session 3 · By-hand design runs (build guide step 22, the ladder's first rung)
Three Opus sub-agents ran the article's p.14 prompt verbatim on fixture copies (a percent helper, a remote exchange-rates module with an API token, a Node CLI with JSON output). Results: specs of 1009 / 1740 / 1741 words with the five sections in order and 6 / 6 / 9 open concerns each. The prompt in `/sdlc-design` (v1) differs from the article's because of them: it names the five sections; states the `[x]`/`closed`/`resolved`/`decided` convention and that the closing word must be the first word of the item (a `C3:` label keeps a concern open); demands every open question answered or carried forward with its source; turns assumptions into concerns; replaces "brand guidelines, security policies and UX standards" (three of five skills, and it calls the output "the plan") with "every organization skill, apply or say why not, cite the rule"; drops "ready to hand to the engineering team", which pulled files and order of work into the spec in all three runs; says read-only Read/Grep/Glob with a single write; and asks for `CLAUDE.md` and `sdlc.yaml` to be read and flagged when missing. Kept verbatim: "especially where you cannot satisfy contradicting policies" — it produced the real contradictions (coding standards versus UX wording in error messages; security's "validate against a schema" versus "no new dependency"). Skill effects: coding-standards and security-baseline load-bearing; ux-conventions found the true contradictions even for library changes but half of it assumes screens; data-conventions matters only when something is stored; definition-of-done shapes Acceptance. The intents lacked precision (rounding mode, exit codes, API shape): the intent template could ask for examples — noted for the owner, not changed.

## Session 3 · Known gaps (carried to B4; the session-4 section above says which are closed)
- The workflows, `run_phase.py` against a real `claude`, the check-run posting and the digest have never run live (no runner and no live `claude -p` in the session); every flag is quoted from the docs (NOTES §11) and tested with a fake CLI.
- Which `permissions:` key each GitHub endpoint needs was not verifiable (docs.github.com blocked from the session); the workflows request five keys.
- The framework repository carries no release tag yet; the phase jobs need `v0.2.0` on the merge commit of PR #10.
- The Full-profile approval label cannot be verified without `gh` or a token: the job then skips (it never runs unapproved); only the actor check degrades with a printed note.
- `gate.max_budget_usd` binds unattended runs only (spend is known from the `claude -p` result); by hand, iterations and the wall clock are the limits.
- On Claude Code on the web, `/sdlc-plan` and `/sdlc-init` push to the platform's assigned branch, not to `sdlc/<id>/a`; the merge-triggered design workflow keys on a head named `sdlc/<id>/a`, so an intent PR opened from the web will not start the design run until the branch rule is adapted (B4: dispatch by change id from the merged `status.yaml` instead of the head name, or push `sdlc/<id>/a` as a second ref).
- `state/cli.py commit-phase` stages every path it is given, including unchanged protected files such as `.claude/settings.json` listed by `/sdlc-init`; a session's permission classifier may deny that. B4: stage only changed paths.
- Monthly tuning of review findings (26.5) is not built.
- The adversarial reviewer still has `Bash` (session 2's gap); the protected-path hook and the review pass remain the nets. Risk-list matching stays lexical, now over the diff paths and the spec's "Flagged concerns" section only (0.2.5); policy checks stay advisory.

## Session 3 · Live checks the owner can do now (not automatable here)
Test repository: `luissiviero/sdlc-sample-python` (plugin loaded through the setup script of NOTES §3a). Session 2's checks 2–6 were unreported at the start of session 3 and are still owed.
1. Tag the framework: `git tag v0.2.0 <merge commit of PR #10> && git push origin v0.2.0`.
2. Store `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (from `claude setup-token`) as a repository secret in both repositories; run Actions → "Substrate smoke test" in `sdlc-framework`: green closes build guide step 3.
3. Re-run `/sdlc:sdlc-init` in the sample repo: expect `.github/workflows/sdlc-*.yml`, `.github/scripts/sdlc_pin.py`, the `test_paths` and `plugin.claude_code` keys added to `sdlc.yaml`, everything else `unchanged` / `kept`; merge the change-0000 PR it opens.
4. Set the sample repository's watch to "Participating and @mentions" (the Watch dropdown; Custom is only for adding events on top of it).
5. Agent triggers (session 2's check 4): "run the adversarial reviewer for phase c of change 0001" must write `evidence/adversarial-review-c.json`, and `gate/cli.py check --id 0001 --phase c` must read it.
6. End-to-end. First, once per repository: Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions → tick "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests" → Save (NOTES §11a; without it every phase job ends with "GitHub Actions is not permitted to create or approve pull requests", as the fifth live run did). Then `/sdlc:sdlc-plan "<idea>"` → merge the intent PR → the design workflow opens the spec+plan PR (`sdlc:b-ready` or `sdlc:needs-human`). From a web session the intent PR's head is the platform's branch, not `sdlc/<id>/a`, so the merge does not fire the workflow (gap above): start it by hand instead — Actions → "sdlc-design" → Run workflow → `change_id` = the four-digit id — which runs the same `run_phase.py` path. Paste the job's auth line, `total_cost_usd`, the gate result and the PR link into session 4's first message.
7. Windows: `python tasks.py check` on the PC (the session touched path handling), and an attempted edit of a test file on a fix-type change branch should show the "Test-file lock" denial.
8. Set `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=opus` in the cloud environment's variables before session 4.

## Session 3 · Results of the live checks so far (2026-09-21 to 2026-09-23)
- Smoke run in `sdlc-framework`: green in 16 s; `auth: subscription-token`, model `claude-opus-5`, `total_cost_usd: 0.0089815`, reply `OK`. Build guide step 3 closed. Annotations: actions/checkout@v4 and setup-node@v4 are on Node 20 (deprecated; bump to v5 in B4); `ubuntu-latest` moves to Ubuntu 26 on 2026-10-19.
- `/sdlc:sdlc-init` re-run on `sdlc-sample-python` with plugin 0.2.0 (environment setup script rebuilt): pin 0.1.0 → 0.2.0, `test_paths`, `plugin.claude_code`, the five workflows, `sdlc_pin.py`, `bands.yaml`, `evals/` created; comments kept; the three targets green; draft PR #4 opened with `sdlc:a-ready`. The upgrade path fixed in this session works live.
- First live design run on `sdlc-sample-python` (workflow_dispatch, change 0001, framework at `v0.2.0`): checkout, pin step, CLI install and the sandbox setup (bubblewrap, socat, AppArmor profile) all worked on the runner; `claude -p` then refused with "Input must be provided either through stdin or as a prompt argument" because `--allowedTools`/`--disallowedTools` take a list of values and the prompt placed after them was read as a tool name. Fixed in 0.2.1 (`run_phase.compose` puts the prompt right after `-p`; regression test). Also observed: GitHub Actions refused to start the job while the sample repository was private (billing); the owner made it public.
- Second live design run (framework `v0.2.1`, run 3 of the workflow): the substrate worked end to end — checkout, CLI, sandbox, the headless `/sdlc-design 0001` pass (spec.md, plan.md, adversarial verdict continue, no open concern; 10.7 min, 2.20 USD of subscription usage on the token path). Gate (b) parked for two framework reasons, fixed in 0.2.2: the runner had no pytest/ruff (`sdlc.yaml: commands.setup` + `ci/project_setup.py`, run by every phase job), and the sandbox's placeholder dotfiles plus the `framework/` checkout counted as changes outside the change folder (`.git/info/exclude` written by `run_phase.py`; `design_scope` and `clean_tree` ignore zero-byte untracked placeholders). The parked PR was not opened by the model's session → `run_phase.ensure_pr()` upserts it deterministically. The branch `sdlc/0001/b` holds the artifacts.
- Third live design run (framework `v0.2.2`, GitHub-hosted runner): gate (b) parked again, for three framework reasons, fixed in 0.2.3. (i) The gate is executed by the model inside Claude Code's sandbox, which masks `.env`, `.idea` and `.vscode`: they showed as modified or untracked although `.git/info/exclude` carried them and the committed diff held only the change folder — `design_scope` now judges `git diff <merge base>...HEAD` and only reports the working tree, `clean_tree` ignores `diff.SANDBOX_MASKED` at the root, and the exclude entries lost their trailing slash so a *file* placeholder matches too. (ii) The project's installed workflow files were still the 0.2.0 copies (create-only install), so the toolchain step never ran and `check_commands` failed with "No module named pytest" — `run_phase.py` now runs `commands.setup` itself before the guard. (iii) `ensure_pr` reported `ok` for an upsert that had opened no PR: `_cli_call` discarded stdout, so the upsert's own route/url/reason were lost. It keeps the JSON now, `ok` means route `gh` or `api` with a number or url, and a run that ends with no PR exits 1. Also in 0.2.3: the token route is tried before `gh`, and `gh` runs with the project root as its working directory.
- Fourth live design run (framework `v0.2.3`): the toolchain step ran first and failed on its own default — `pip install -e .` triggers setuptools' flat-layout discovery, which refuses a project directory holding `changes/`, `evals/`, the `framework/` checkout and the package (the framework repository hit the same in PR #8). Fixed in 0.2.4: no editable install unless the project declares an extra; the default is `python -m pip install pytest ruff`.
- Fifth live design run (framework `v0.2.4`, run 35668204108): the whole phase worked — toolchain, `claude -p` (6.3 min, 1.15 USD), spec.md and plan.md, the adversarial verdict, gate (b), the evidence pushed on `sdlc/0001/b` — and the job failed only at the PR: `GitHub Actions is not permitted to create or approve pull requests`, a repository setting that was never turned on (NOTES §11a; the owner's live check 6 below). Two framework faults showed in the gate file and are fixed in 0.2.5: (i) `check_risk_list` scanned the whole spec.md, and security-baseline rule 8 makes every spec name the risk items it checked, so every design parked on a false hit — the check now reads the diff paths and the "Flagged concerns" section only (spec-template and the design prompt say where a risk item is declared); (ii) the adversarial reviewer re-ran the gate's own dry run and escalated on its output — its prompt now forbids repeating the deterministic checks. Also in 0.2.5: `HEAD_REF` in the phase workflows falls back to the `workflow_dispatch` input (it was empty on every dispatched run), and `tests/test_ci_end_to_end.py` runs `run_phase.py` as the workflow does, against a fake `claude` that performs the design's Python half and a fake `gh` that answers or refuses like GitHub, so the next fault in this path is found locally. The five runs were one bug each, found live at a plugin release apiece; the end-to-end test is the answer to that.
- Sixth live design run (2026-09-22, run 35674839063, tag `v0.2.5` — which the owner had put on the old `main` before PR #16 merged, so it ran 0.2.4 code): green in 40 s and did nothing: `{"skipped": "change 0001 is at phase b, not a"}`. The run switched to the existing `sdlc/0001/b` of the fifth run, whose `status.yaml` says phase b and parked, and the guard skipped it; with no PR for the owner to comment on, nothing could move the change. Fixed in 0.2.5 (`run_phase.rerun_reason`): a (b) or (c) run whose work branch already carries the phase but has no open pull request is a re-run of an attempt that never reached the queue — the park is cleared and the phase runs again; a branch whose PR exists still skips (review comments go through `/sdlc-fix`), and no GitHub route skips too. Covered by the end-to-end test (park, refused PR, second dispatch opens it) and three guard tests.
- Seventh live design run (2026-09-22, run 35677309939, framework `v0.2.5` on the PR #16 merge): the first end-to-end success — phase (b) ran (1.13 USD), the spend record was committed, PR #5 opened with `sdlc:needs-human`, parked on the one open flagged concern (by design; the risk-list false positive was gone). The owner closed the concern (PR #6 into `sdlc/0001/b`), then two `/sdlc-fix 0001` rounds ran from Claude Code sessions: round 1 (cloud environment, plugin cache still 0.2.0) synced plan.md and re-parked on the old risk-list scan plus a real reviewer finding — `divide(part, whole) * 100` returns `7.000000000000001` for `percent(7, 100)`; round 2 (owner's PC, plugin 0.2.5) applied the owner's decision `divide(part * 100, whole)` with an exact-percentage test, verdict continue, gate (b) `wait`, PR #5 `sdlc:b-ready`, iterations 2 of 2. Defect found in the result: `Status.record_gate` kept the old `parked_reason` under a `passed` result, so the build run's guard would have skipped the merged change as parked — fixed in 0.2.6 (a non-parked gate result lifts the park; the CI guard also ignores a park whose gate has since passed, for branches written by older plugins). Observations for B4: the cloud environment's plugin cache is not refreshed by the setup script's `install` alone (add `marketplace update` + `plugin update`); the reviewer keeps re-running the deterministic checks despite the 0.2.5 wording; there is no CI route for `/sdlc-fix`, so fix rounds run by hand.
- Eighth live run, the first build (c) (2026-09-22, run 35682441638, framework `v0.2.6` on the PR #17 merge, dispatched by hand after the merge of PR #5 had fired a build run that skipped on the stale park): the guard passed (the park was over), the preflight allowed `acceptEdits`, and `claude -p /sdlc:sdlc-build 0001` returned after 28 s, exit 0, no `is_error` — and no `evidence/gate-c.json`, no branch pushed, no PR. `run_phase.py` printed one line and the runner took the CLI's result and stderr with it, so the cause is unknown; a local reproduction was not possible (the CI settings require a sandbox Windows does not provide, and the owner's CLI login had expired). Two changes in 0.2.7: (i) a run that ends without its gate prints the CLI's last message, its counters and its stderr, stores the stderr as `evidence/claude-<phase>.stderr.txt` and commits the record on the work branch; (ii) phases (c), (d) and (e) get an explicit `--allowedTools` list mirroring `settings.ci.json` (as (b) always had), since the project's allow rules are ignored in an untrusted checkout and whether a `--settings` allow-list counts is undocumented — the likeliest cause of a session that gives up at once. Covered by an end-to-end case (a session that gives up; the record reaches the branch; the next dispatch runs the phase) and an argv test that ties the list to the settings file.
- Ninth and tenth live runs, build (c) again (2026-09-22, runs 35682441638 and 35684203219, frameworks `v0.2.6` and `v0.2.7`): the 0.2.7 record showed the cause the eighth run had hidden. The session ran four turns, spent 0.13 USD, returned `is_error: false` and refused in its own words: "Per the preflight instructions, I must stop if `parked_reason` is set on the change - and it is." The design PR had merged `status.yaml` as plugin 0.2.5 wrote it, gate (b) `passed` with the old `parked_reason` beside it; 0.2.6 taught only the CI guard to treat that as a lifted park, and `sdlc-build.md` step 0 still told the model to stop on the raw field. Three readers of one state with three private rules, and the model's rule-following was the one layer no test exercised. Fixed in 0.2.8, test first: (i) `state/status.py` is the one reader - `read_status` drops a `parked_reason` beside any gate result but `parked` (a park is a gate result), so the guard, `show`, `list` and the preflight agree, and `lift_stale_park` rewrites the file on the work branch before the CLI starts, since the session reads it itself; (ii) `gate/preflight.py` gains the park check (with `--id`) and a `--phase` (d and e skip the test target), and the build, test and deploy commands read its `allow` instead of judging `status.yaml`; (iii) the end-to-end fake `claude` now follows step 0 of its command literally (reads the change, stops on a park, asks the preflight for (c)) and performs the recorded build half, and a new case merges the 0.2.5-shaped file into main and expects the (c) job to open the draft PR with `parked_reason: null`. The stderr line "Ignoring 4 permissions.allow entries: this workspace has not been trusted" was not the blocker: the session read files and ran git under the explicit `--allowedTools`. Observation for B4: `run_phase._start_point` starts `sdlc/<id>/c` from `sdlc/<id>/b` whenever that branch still exists, in every profile, not only Lite.
- Eleventh live run, the first build (c) to reach its gate (2026-09-22, run 35688588198, framework `v0.2.8`, after the owner deleted the `sdlc/0001/c` a 0.2.7 session had parked): 23 turns, 2.10 USD, `percent()` in `calc.py`, the export in `__init__.py` and two tests - exactly the three files plan.md lists, one plan step per commit - 5 tests green, verifier.md, adversarial `continue`, gate (c), draft PR #7 with `sdlc:needs-human`. The whole pipeline ran; the park was on `plan_sync` alone, for two framework reasons fixed in 0.2.9, test first: (i) `check_plan_sync` judged `diff.collect().files`, the merge base against the *working tree*, where the sandbox-masked `.env` shows as modified - it now judges `committed_files()` as `check_design_scope` has since 0.2.3, and the working tree stays `check_clean_tree`'s; (ii) the per-commit rule (hook and gate) demanded plan.md in every source commit on `sdlc/<id>/c`, stricter than the prose and article p.16 step 7 - it now fires only when a commit touches a source file "## Files that change" does not list, the hook reading the plan from the change folder; the gate reuses the hook's `is_planned` (which also fixes a directory entry `src/` never matching, because the old matcher stripped the trailing slash first). `claude-c.json` shows `permission_denials: 0`, so the hook denied nothing: the commits were driven from `commit-phase`, which the hook cannot parse (NOTES section 9), and the gate's per-commit re-check is what caught them. Also in 0.2.9: `commit-phase` applies the plan-sync rule itself before committing (exit 2 with the hook's reason), since it is the route the build command prescribes and the hook, registered on `git *` shell commands, never sees it - the denial now arrives at the commit, not twenty turns later. Owner's route for PR #7 once 0.2.9 is pinned: `/sdlc:sdlc-fix 0001` re-runs gate (c) without a new build run.
- Twelfth and thirteenth live runs, test (d) and deploy (e) (2026-09-23, runs 35840525557 and 35841003129, framework `v0.2.9`), after the first `/sdlc-fix 0001` round run headless from the owner's PC (13 turns, 0.83 USD) cleared the plan_sync park and gate (c) said `continue`: (d) collected the three logs, fresh verdict, gate (d) `continue` (0.82 USD) and dispatched (e) itself - the first automated phase transition; (e) ran the review pass and gate (e) (2.04 USD), parked on `limits` only: the review left one nit (the accepted overflow point) and one Important finding, CLAUDE.md's Layout line still names only `add` and `divide`, and the session spent two rounds, the second past the cap of 2 (one round had gone to the (c) fix), trying to keep the proposed line in the PR body, which `review/cli.py validate` deletes when the finding is not a repeat. That finding is a deadlock at gate (e) by construction (only the owner edits CLAUDE.md, a CLAUDE.md commit on the change branch fails `guardrails`, findings cannot be accepted); decision 21 (2026-09-23) routes an undeterminable Important finding to the deferred review panel, so the exit belongs to that build. In the Standard profile the owner's merge of PR #7 is gate (e). Two smaller findings, documented in the docs follow-up after 0.2.10 (no version bump): (i) the fix-iteration counter is one per change (OPERATING_MODEL §4.1), while the comments in `template/sdlc.yaml` and `limits.py` said "per phase" - the comments were wrong, not the code, and they now say per change; the defaults stay 3 and 2 (non-routine), so one change spends its rounds at (c), (d) and (e) together, as 0001 did (one at (c), two at (e), the second past the non-routine cap of 2); after a cap park the owner's exit is the `sdlc:reset-iterations` label or `gate/cli.py set-iterations` (decision 24), and raising a project's cap is the owner's edit of the `gate` block in that project's own `sdlc.yaml`; (ii) the headless fix session chained shell commands (`cd ... && python ...`); a chain runs only when every part passes the explicit tool list, these were denied whole and the session lost its last step - every unattended command now says "one shell command per Bash call" (NOTES section 11). The framework repository's own `sdlc.yaml` (PR #21, self-hosted) still carries the old comment: a protected path, the owner's edit.
- Plugin 0.2.10 (2026-09-23, no live run behind it): with the framework installed into its own repository (PR #21, change 0000), the protected-path hook denied `template/CLAUDE.md`, `template/REVIEW.md` and `template/sdlc.yaml`, because a pattern without a slash matched its basename in any directory. The four always-protected patterns are now anchored to the project root (`/.claude/**`, `/CLAUDE.md`, `/REVIEW.md`, `/sdlc.yaml`); the owner's `protected_paths` keep gitignore semantics; the gate's `check_guardrails` and the CI branch guard share the patterns and follow. Reasoning, the accepted trade-off (nested `CLAUDE.md` files are project content unless listed) and the residual (the `Edit(...)` deny rules in the settings files match by basename too, and stay for the owner) in NOTES section 12. Tests: nested names free and root names caught, on Windows spellings too; the branch guard on `template/CLAUDE.md`.
- Follow-up the same day (no version bump; ships with the next release): the `Edit(...)` deny rules matched by basename too (the permissions reference: bare filenames follow gitignore semantics). The template spells the four rules with the `/` project-root anchor; `settings.ci.json` does too and `run_phase.ci_settings_file` passes a temporary copy rewritten to `//<absolute project root>/...`, since a `/path` rule in a `--settings` file anchors at the file's own directory; the preflight and the security-baseline check accept either spelling. Left to the owner: the same four lines in this repository's root `.claude/settings.json`; the managed file keeps the bare names (NOTES sections 6 and 12).
- Close-out of change 0001 and the docs follow-up (2026-09-23 afternoon). **PR #30** (documentation only, no version bump): the cap comments in `template/sdlc.yaml` and `limits.py` said "per phase" while OPERATING_MODEL §4.1 and the code count one counter per change; they now say per change and the defaults stay 3 and 2 (a default change is a decision, not a docs PR); build guide step 19 and the adversarial reviewer say the same, with a test over the three prose copies; the five unattended commands say "One shell command per Bash call" (the permissions reference matches each part of a chain on its own, so a chain with one uncovered part is denied whole), with a test; NOTES §11 and the twelfth/thirteenth-run bullet above record the observations. Alongside it the owner fixed the same comment in this repository's root `sdlc.yaml` (8fe9a97, directly on `main`), then merged the sample repository's PR #7 at 16:17 UTC as gate (e) (Standard profile; the merge-triggered build and design runs skipped with "head branch sdlc/0001/c is phase c, not b" (build) and "head branch sdlc/0001/c is phase c, not a" (design)), and edited the sample repository's `CLAUDE.md` Layout line (`percent` listed, the Important finding of the thirteenth run closed) and its `sdlc.yaml` cap comment on `main`. Two residues for B4: the sample repository's `status.yaml` on `main` still says phase e, gate parked, iterations 3, because nothing records an owner's merge as gate (e) passed; and its pin is still 0.2.9.
